\emph{Step 5: comparison.}  Insert \eqref{eq:qe-right-expansion} and
\eqref{eq:qe-left-expansion} into \eqref{eq:qe-envelope}:
\[
 -s\,\langle A,g\rangle+o(|s|)\le-s\,\langle A,Du^{\mathsf T}q\rangle+o(|s|)
 \qquad(0<|s|\le s_0).
\]
Dividing by $s>0$ and letting $s\downarrow0$ gives
$\langle A,g\rangle\ge\langle A,Du^{\mathsf T}q\rangle$; dividing by $s<0$
(which reverses the inequality) and letting $s\uparrow0$ gives
$\langle A,g\rangle\le\langle A,Du^{\mathsf T}q\rangle$.  Hence
$\langle A,(u\cdot\nabla)u\rangle=\langle A,Du^{\mathsf T}q\rangle$, which is
\eqref{eq:qe-transport}--\eqref{eq:qe-contraction} by
\eqref{eq:qe-right-expansion}.  The absolute convergence statements are
H\"older's inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[No derivative of the minimizer]\label{rem:qe-transport-scope}
No derivative of $q$ or $w$ appears in the proof: the competitor $q_s$ is
the pullback of the fixed $L^3$ field $q$, and the two-sided comparison at
$s=0$ replaces the differentiation of the minimizer.  No smoothness of the
minimizer is used or asserted.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Evolution of the quotient]\label{prop:quotient-evolution}
With the notation and package (R) of Section~\ref{subsec:qe-trajectories},
$t\mapsto\mathcal Q(u(t))$ belongs to $C^1([0,T])$; the functions
$t\mapsto D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))$ and
$t\mapsto\int q(t)\cdot\bigl((A(t)\cdot\nabla)u(t)\bigr)dx$ are continuous
on $[0,T]$; and for every $t\in[0,T]$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:quotient-evolution}
 \frac d{dt}\mathcal Q(u(t))+\nu D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))
 =-\int_{\R^3}q(t)\cdot\bigl((A(t)\cdot\nabla)u(t)\bigr)dx,
 \qquad D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))\ge0 .
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-chainrule}, $\mathcal Q\circ u\in C^1([0,T])$
and
$\frac d{dt}\mathcal Q(u(t))=\nu\langle A,\Delta u\rangle-\langle A,(u\cdot\nabla)u\rangle$;
the pressure term has already been cancelled there by
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-pressure}.  By Definition~\ref{def:qe-dissipation},
$\nu\langle A,\Delta u\rangle=-\nu D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))$, with
$D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))\ge0$ and continuous by
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-heatsign}.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-transport},
$\langle A,(u\cdot\nabla)u\rangle=\int q\cdot((A\cdot\nabla)u)$.  Combining
gives \eqref{eq:quotient-evolution}.  For the continuity of the right
side: $q(t)=w(t)-u(t)$ is continuous into $L^3$ by
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-stability}, estimate \eqref{eq:cp-strong},
and $u\in C([0,T];L^3)$; $A\in C([0,T];L^{3/2})$ by the same estimate;
$Du\in C([0,T];L^\infty)$ by (R3); and for $q,q'\in L^3$,
$A,A'\in L^{3/2}$, $v,v'$ with bounded gradients,
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \Bigl|\int q\cdot((A\cdot\nabla)v)-\int q'\cdot((A'\cdot\nabla)v')\Bigr|
 &\le\norm{q-q'}_3\norm A_{3/2}\norm{Dv}_\infty
 +\norm{q'}_3\norm{A-A'}_{3/2}\norm{Dv}_\infty\\
 &\quad+\norm{q'}_3\norm{A'}_{3/2}\norm{Dv-Dv'}_\infty ,
\end{aligned}
\]
by H\"older's inequality with exponents $3$, $3/2$, $\infty$ and the
pointwise bound $|q\cdot((A\cdot\nabla)v)|=|q\cdot(Dv\,A)|\le|q|\,|Dv|\,|A|$
from \eqref{eq:qe-jacobian}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Content of the evolution identity]\label{rem:qe-evolution-scope}
Identity \eqref{eq:quotient-evolution} contains no pressure term and no
derivative of the minimizer.  It is exact on every compact classical
interval.  It supplies neither a sign nor a bound for its right side.
\end{remark}

% ---------- Block D (i): the sharper transport bound ----------

\begin{remark}[Attribution of the weighted objects]
\label{rem:qe-weighted-related}
None of the objects in Lemma~\ref{lem:qe-weighted-dissipation} is new, and the
names they carry elsewhere are recorded here rather than left implicit.  The
substitution $V=|w|^{1/2}w$ is the standard $V$-function of degenerate
quasilinear theory, $V(z)=|z|^{(p-2)/2}z$ at $p=3$; that $p$-harmonic maps
satisfy $|\nabla u|^{(p-2)/2}\nabla u\in W^{1,2}_{\rm loc}$ for $p\ge2$, proved
by difference quotients, is classical \cite[Thm.~4.1]{Lindqvist2019}, and the
regularity theory for the associated systems is due to Uhlenbeck
\cite{Uhlenbeck1977}.  The weighted quantity
$\int\rho\,|\partial_kw|^2$ and the two-sided comparison
\eqref{eq:qe-pointwise-V} belong to a single well-named equivalence class: it is
the \emph{quasi-norm} of Barrett and Liu \cite{BarrettLiu1994}, the
\emph{shifted $N$-function} of Diening and Ettwein \cite{DieningEttwein2008},
and the \emph{natural distance} of the numerical $p$-Dirichlet literature
\cite[Rem.~2.6]{Kaltenbach2024}, which uses that phrase for all three
equivalent quantities; it is also the Bregman divergence of the integrand
$\tfrac13|z|^3$.  What a bounded literature search did not locate is the
combination used here: the shifted problem, posed against the whole closed
gradient subspace of $L^3(\R^3)$ rather than a cohomology class, with a global
rather than local conclusion.  That is a statement about the reach of the
search, not a claim of priority, and no novelty is asserted for the mechanism.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Strain work against the weighted dissipation]
\label{lem:qe-strain-defect}
Let $m\ge4$ be an integer and let $u\in H^m(\R^3;\R^3)$ be solenoidal, with
$w=w(u)$, $q=q(u)$, $A=A(u)$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-minimizer}(b),
$V=|w|^{1/2}w$ and $D_{\mathcal Q}(u)$ as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:qe-weighted-dissipation}.  Then, with $C_9$ the constant of
\textup{(F5)} and $C_S$ that of Definition~\ref{def:sobolev-constant},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-strain-defect}
 |K_u|\ \le\ \tfrac32\,C_9C_S\,\norm q_3\,D_{\mathcal Q}(u).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Step~3 of Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-mixed-pressure} with $b=u$,
\[
 K_u=-\int_{\R^3}q\cdot F_u\,dx,
 \qquad
 (F_u)_j=u_i\partial_iA_j ,
\]
the integral converging absolutely (that step bounds $|q||u||\nabla A|$ by
H\"older with exponents $3,\infty,\frac32$).  Hence
$|K_u|\le\int|q|\,|u|\,|\nabla A|_F$.

Write $A=\Phi_{1/3}(V)$ with $\Phi_{1/3}(z)=|z|^{1/3}z$: indeed
$|V|^{1/3}V=|w|^{1/2}\cdot|w|^{1/2}w=|w|w=A$.  Away from the origin
$D\Phi_{1/3}(z)=|z|^{1/3}I+\frac13|z|^{-5/3}z\otimes z$, so
$|D\Phi_{1/3}(z)|\le\frac43|z|^{1/3}$, and
$|\Phi_{1/3}(z)|=|z|^{4/3}=o(|z|)$ gives $D\Phi_{1/3}(0)=0$; thus
$\Phi_{1/3}\in C^1$ with $\Phi_{1/3}(0)=0$ and
$|D\Phi_{1/3}(z)|\le\frac43|z|^{1/3}\le\frac43(1+|z|)$.  Since
$V\in H^1\subset L^6$ with $\nabla V\in L^2$ by
Lemma~\ref{lem:qe-weighted-dissipation}, the chain rule
\eqref{eq:qdc-chainrule} applies with $\Psi=\Phi_{1/3}$ and $z=V$ and gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-Agrad-V}
 |\nabla A|_F\ \le\ \tfrac43|V|^{1/3}|\nabla V|_F
 =\tfrac43|w|^{1/2}|\nabla V|_F\qquad\text{a.e.}
\end{equation}
H\"older's inequality with the four exponents $3,9,18,2$, whose reciprocals
sum to $\frac6{18}+\frac2{18}+\frac1{18}+\frac9{18}=1$, therefore gives
\[
 |K_u|\le\tfrac43\norm q_3\,\norm u_9\,\bigl\||w|^{1/2}\bigr\|_{18}\,\norm{\nabla V}_2
 =\tfrac43\norm q_3\,\norm u_9\,\norm w_9^{1/2}\,\norm{\nabla V}_2 ,
\]
since $\bigl\||w|^{1/2}\bigr\|_{18}=\bigl(\int|w|^9\bigr)^{1/18}=\norm w_9^{1/2}$.
By \textup{(F5)} and $\mathbb Pw=u$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-coercive}),
$\norm u_9\le C_9\norm w_9$, so
\[
 |K_u|\le\tfrac43C_9\norm q_3\,\norm w_9^{3/2}\,\norm{\nabla V}_2 .
\]
By \eqref{eq:qe-weighted-coercive}, $\norm w_9^{3/2}=\norm V_6\le C_S\norm{\nabla V}_2$
and $\norm{\nabla V}_2^2\le\frac98D_{\mathcal Q}(u)$, whence
\[
 |K_u|\le\tfrac43C_9C_S\norm q_3\norm{\nabla V}_2^2
 \le\tfrac43\cdot\tfrac98\,C_9C_S\norm q_3D_{\mathcal Q}(u)
 =\tfrac32C_9C_S\norm q_3D_{\mathcal Q}(u).\qedhere
\]
\end{proof}

% ---------- Block D (ii): the criterion ----------

\begin{proposition}[A divergence-defect differential inequality]
\label{prop:qe-defect-criterion}
Let $\nu>0$, let $u_0\in\mathcal S(\R^3)^3$ be divergence free, let $(u,p)$
be the maximal classical branch on $[0,T_*)$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:localtheory}, and let $[0,T]\subset[0,T_*)$.  Write
$Q(t)=\mathcal Q(u(t))$, $D_{\mathcal Q}(t)=D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))$ and
$\sigma(t)$ for the divergence defect \eqref{eq:qdc-sigma} of $u(t)$.  With
$a_0=\frac98C_S^2$ as in \eqref{eq:qe-a0}, put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-Csigma}
 C_\sigma:=\frac{81}{32}\,C_6^4\,a_0^3
 =\frac{81}{32}\,C_6^4\Bigl(\frac{9C_S^2}8\Bigr)^{3}.
\end{equation}
Then, for every $t\in[0,T]$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-defect-diff}
 Q'(t)+\frac\nu2D_{\mathcal Q}(t)
 \ \le\ C_\sigma\,\nu^{-3}\norm{\sigma(t)}_2^4\,Q(t).
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Fix $t$ and drop it from the notation; $u=u(t)$ lies in $H^m$ for every
$m$ and is solenoidal by (R1), so
Lemmas~\ref{lem:quotient-mixed-pressure} and
\ref{lem:qe-weighted-dissipation} apply.

\emph{Step 1: entry.}  By \eqref{eq:qdc-mixedpair} with $b=u$ and the
Cauchy--Schwarz inequality, followed by the left bound of
\eqref{eq:qdc-mixedbound},
\[
 |K_u|=\Bigl|\int\sigma\,\Pi_u\Bigr|
 \le\norm\sigma_2\norm{\Pi_u}_2
 \le\norm\sigma_2\norm u_6\norm w_6^2 .
\]
By \textup{(F5)} with $p=6$ and $\mathbb Pw=u$,
$\norm u_6\le C_6\norm w_6$, so
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-entry}
 |K_u|\le C_6\norm\sigma_2\norm w_6^3 .
\end{equation}

\emph{Step 2: interpolation between the cubic and the ninth-power norm.}
Since $\frac16=\frac{\theta}3+\frac{1-\theta}9$ has the unique solution
$\theta=\frac14$, H\"older's inequality gives
$\norm w_6\le\norm w_3^{1/4}\norm w_9^{3/4}$, hence
\[
 \norm w_6^3\le\norm w_3^{3/4}\norm w_9^{9/4}
 =\bigl(\norm w_3^3\bigr)^{1/4}\bigl(\norm w_9^3\bigr)^{3/4}
 \le(3Q)^{1/4}\bigl(a_0D_{\mathcal Q}\bigr)^{3/4},
\]
using $\norm w_3^3=3\mathcal Q(u)=3Q$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-minimizer}(b))
and $\norm w_9^3\le a_0D_{\mathcal Q}$
\eqref{eq:qe-weighted-coercive}.  With
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-B0}
 B_0:=C_6\,3^{1/4}a_0^{3/4},
\end{equation}
Step~1 becomes
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-preyoung}
 |K_u|\ \le\ B_0\,\norm\sigma_2\,Q^{1/4}D_{\mathcal Q}^{3/4}.
\end{equation}

\emph{Step 3: the exact Young maximisation.}  For $\alpha,\varepsilon>0$
the function $x\mapsto\alpha x^{3/4}-\varepsilon x$ on $[0,\infty)$ is
differentiable on $(0,\infty)$ with derivative
$\frac34\alpha x^{-1/4}-\varepsilon$, which vanishes exactly at
$x_*=\bigl(\frac{3\alpha}{4\varepsilon}\bigr)^4$ and is positive before and
negative after it; the function is continuous at $0$ and tends to $-\infty$,
so $x_*$ is its global maximum, with value
\[
 \alpha x_*^{3/4}-\varepsilon x_*
 =\alpha\Bigl(\frac{3\alpha}{4\varepsilon}\Bigr)^3
 -\varepsilon\Bigl(\frac{3\alpha}{4\varepsilon}\Bigr)^4
 =\frac{27\alpha^4}{64\varepsilon^3}-\frac{81\alpha^4}{256\varepsilon^3}
 =\frac{27\alpha^4}{256\,\varepsilon^3}.
\]
Hence $\alpha x^{3/4}\le\varepsilon x+\frac{27\alpha^4}{256\varepsilon^3}$
for all $x\ge0$, with equality at $x=x_*$: the constant is exact, not
merely an upper bound.  Take $\alpha=B_0\norm\sigma_2Q^{1/4}$,
$x=D_{\mathcal Q}\ge0$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-heatsign}) and
$\varepsilon=\frac\nu2$ in \eqref{eq:qe-preyoung}:
\[
 |K_u|\ \le\ \frac\nu2D_{\mathcal Q}
 +\frac{27\,B_0^4\norm\sigma_2^4\,Q}{256\,(\nu/2)^3}
 =\frac\nu2D_{\mathcal Q}+\frac{27}{32}B_0^4\,\nu^{-3}\norm\sigma_2^4\,Q .
\]
By \eqref{eq:qe-B0}, $B_0^4=3C_6^4a_0^3$, so
$\frac{27}{32}B_0^4=\frac{81}{32}C_6^4a_0^3=C_\sigma$, which is exactly
\eqref{eq:qe-Csigma}.

\emph{Step 4: the evolution identity.}  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-evolution}, $Q\in C^1([0,T])$ and
$Q'+\nu D_{\mathcal Q}=K_u$ pointwise on $[0,T]$, the right side being the
strain work of \eqref{eq:quotient-evolution} identified with $K_u$ in
Step~5 of Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-mixed-pressure}.  Subtracting
$\frac\nu2D_{\mathcal Q}$ from both sides of
$Q'+\nu D_{\mathcal Q}=K_u\le|K_u|$ and inserting Step~3 gives
\eqref{eq:qe-defect-diff}.
\end{proof}

% ---------- Block D (iii): the Gronwall corollary ----------

\begin{corollary}[Integrated form]\label{cor:qe-defect-gronwall}
In the setting of Proposition~\ref{prop:qe-defect-criterion} put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-Lambda}
 \Lambda_\sigma(t):=C_\sigma\,\nu^{-3}\int_0^t\norm{\sigma(s)}_2^4\,ds
 \qquad(0\le t\le T),
\end{equation}
which is written $\Lambda_\sigma$ to keep it apart from the constant
$\Lambda$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:flow}.  Then $\Lambda_\sigma$ is finite,
nondecreasing and Lipschitz on $[0,T]$, and for every $t\in[0,T]$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-defect-integral}
 Q(t)+\frac\nu2\int_0^tD_{\mathcal Q}(s)\,ds
 \ \le\ Q(0)\,e^{\Lambda_\sigma(t)} .
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
\emph{The coefficient.}  By the measurability argument in the proof of
Corollary~\ref{cor:quotient-budgets} --- $t\mapsto w(t)$ is continuous into
$L^3$, and $\norm{\operatorname{div}w(t)}_2$ is the supremum of the
countably many continuous functions
$t\mapsto-\int w(t)\cdot\nabla\eta$, $\eta\in\mathcal E$ --- the map
$t\mapsto\norm{\sigma(t)}_2$ is Borel on $[0,T]$.  By
\eqref{eq:qdc-sigmaid}, $\norm{\sigma(t)}_2^2\le\frac14Y(t)$ with
$Y(t)=\norm{\nabla u(t)}_2^2$ continuous on the compact interval $[0,T]$ by
(R1); hence $\norm{\sigma(\cdot)}_2^4$ is Borel and bounded on $[0,T]$, so
$\Lambda_\sigma$ is finite and Lipschitz there, in particular absolutely
continuous, with $\Lambda_\sigma'=C_\sigma\nu^{-3}\norm\sigma_2^4\ge0$
a.e.; and $\Lambda_\sigma$ is nondecreasing.  No bound on
$\int_0^T\norm\sigma_2^4$ beyond finiteness on a compact classical
interval is claimed here, and none is available uniformly in $T$.

\emph{Gronwall.}  $Q\in C^1([0,T])$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-evolution}, so $Qe^{-\Lambda_\sigma}$ is
absolutely continuous on $[0,T]$ and, a.e.,
\[
 \bigl(Qe^{-\Lambda_\sigma}\bigr)'
 =e^{-\Lambda_\sigma}\bigl(Q'-\Lambda_\sigma'Q\bigr)
 \le e^{-\Lambda_\sigma}\Bigl(-\frac\nu2D_{\mathcal Q}\Bigr)
\]
by \eqref{eq:qe-defect-diff}.  Integrating over $[0,t]$ and multiplying by
$e^{\Lambda_\sigma(t)}>0$,
\[
 Q(t)+\frac\nu2\int_0^te^{\Lambda_\sigma(t)-\Lambda_\sigma(s)}D_{\mathcal Q}(s)\,ds
 \ \le\ Q(0)e^{\Lambda_\sigma(t)} .
\]
Because $\Lambda_\sigma$ is nondecreasing,
$\Lambda_\sigma(t)-\Lambda_\sigma(s)\ge0$ for $0\le s\le t$, so the
exponential under the integral is at least $1$; since
$D_{\mathcal Q}\ge0$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-heatsign}), dropping it
gives \eqref{eq:qe-defect-integral}.
\end{proof}

% ---------- Block D (iv): the conditional producer ----------

\begin{corollary}[Conditional producer for the joint strain quantity]
\label{cor:qe-defect-producer}
Let $\nu>0$, $u_0\in\mathcal S(\R^3)^3$ divergence free, $0<H<\infty$, and
write $Q_0=\mathcal Q(u_0)$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-joint-work}
 G(\tau)=\int_0^\tau\norm{q(t)}_3\,D_{\mathcal Q}(t)\,dt .
\end{equation}
Suppose there is a finite $B_\sigma=B_\sigma(\nu,u_0,H)$, obtained without
using any continuation norm of the branch, such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-defect-hypothesis}
 \int_0^\tau\norm{\sigma(t)}_2^4\,dt\ \le\ B_\sigma
 \qquad\text{for every }0<\tau<\min\{H,T_*\}.
\end{equation}
Put $\Lambda_H=C_\sigma\nu^{-3}B_\sigma$.  Then, for every
$0<\tau<\min\{H,T_*\}$,
\begin{align}
 \sup_{0\le t<\min\{H,T_*\}}\mathcal Q(u(t))
 &\ \le\ Q_0\,e^{\Lambda_H},
 \label{eq:qe-producer-Q}\\
 \int_0^\tau D_{\mathcal Q}(t)\,dt
 &\ \le\ \frac{2Q_0}{\nu}\,e^{\Lambda_H},
 \label{eq:qe-producer-D}\\
 G(\tau)
 &\ \le\ \frac{2(1+C_{\mathbb P})3^{1/3}}{\nu}\,Q_0^{4/3}\,
 e^{4\Lambda_H/3} .
 \label{eq:qe-producer-G}
\end{align}
All three right sides depend only on $\nu$, $u_0$ and $H$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
If $Q_0=0$ then $\norm{u_0}_3^3\le3C_{\mathbb P}^3Q_0=0$ by
\eqref{eq:cp-coercive}, so $u_0=0$, the selected branch is identically
zero, $T_*=\infty$, and all three estimates read $0\le0$.  Assume
$Q_0>0$.

Fix $\tau<\min\{H,T_*\}$ and apply
Corollary~\ref{cor:qe-defect-gronwall} on $[0,T]$ with
$\tau\le T<T_*$.  By \eqref{eq:qe-defect-hypothesis},
$\Lambda_\sigma(t)\le\Lambda_H$ for $t\le\tau$, so
\eqref{eq:qe-defect-integral} gives both
$Q(t)\le Q_0e^{\Lambda_H}$ for $t\le\tau$ and
$\frac\nu2\int_0^\tau D_{\mathcal Q}\le Q_0e^{\Lambda_H}$; since
$\tau<\min\{H,T_*\}$ was arbitrary, these are
\eqref{eq:qe-producer-Q} and \eqref{eq:qe-producer-D}.

For \eqref{eq:qe-producer-G}, Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-coercive} gives
$\norm{q(t)}_3\le(1+C_{\mathbb P})\bigl(3\mathcal Q(u(t))\bigr)^{1/3}$, so
by \eqref{eq:qe-producer-Q} and \eqref{eq:qe-producer-D},
\[
 G(\tau)\le\Bigl(\sup_{t\le\tau}\norm{q(t)}_3\Bigr)\int_0^\tau D_{\mathcal Q}
 \le(1+C_{\mathbb P})\bigl(3Q_0e^{\Lambda_H}\bigr)^{1/3}
 \cdot\frac{2Q_0}{\nu}e^{\Lambda_H}
 =\frac{2(1+C_{\mathbb P})3^{1/3}}{\nu}Q_0^{4/3}e^{4\Lambda_H/3}. \qedhere
\]
\end{proof}

% ---------- Block D: the critical family, as a remark ----------

\begin{remark}[A critical family of defect exponents]
\label{rem:qe-defect-family}
The exponent pair carried by \eqref{eq:qe-defect-hypothesis} is
$L^4_tL^2_x$ for $\sigma$, and it sits on a one-parameter line.  Let
$\frac32<a<\infty$, $a'=\frac a{a-1}$, and put
\[
 k=3a',\qquad \alpha=\frac3{2a}\in(0,1),
 \qquad s=\frac1{1-\alpha}=\frac{2a}{2a-3} .
\]
Then $3<k<9$, so $w\in L^k$ by interpolation between
$\norm w_3$ and $\norm w_9$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qe-weighted-dissipation}, and
$\frac1k=\frac{1-\alpha}3+\frac\alpha9$ is an identity.  Repeating the
proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:qe-defect-criterion} with the pairing
$\int\sigma\Pi_u$ estimated in $L^a\times L^{a'}$ instead of
$L^2\times L^2$, and with the general maximisation
$\max_{x\ge0}(\alpha_0x^\alpha-\varepsilon x)
=(1-\alpha)\alpha^{\alpha/(1-\alpha)}\varepsilon^{-\alpha/(1-\alpha)}
\alpha_0^{1/(1-\alpha)}$ at $\varepsilon=\frac\nu2$, gives
\[
 Q'+\frac\nu2D_{\mathcal Q}
 \le C_a\,\nu^{-\alpha/(1-\alpha)}\norm{\sigma}_a^s\,Q,
 \qquad
 C_a=(1-\alpha)\alpha^{\frac\alpha{1-\alpha}}2^{\frac\alpha{1-\alpha}}
 B_a^{\frac1{1-\alpha}},
 \quad
 B_a=C_{T,a'}C_k3^{1-\alpha}a_0^\alpha,
\]
at those times for which $\sigma(t)\in L^a$, where $C_k$ is the constant of
\textup{(F5)} at exponent $k$ and $C_{T,a'}$ is the norm on $L^{a'}$ of the
tensor-to-scalar multiplier $T\mapsto R_iR_jT_{ij}$.  The exponents satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-defect-scaling}
 \frac2s+\frac3a=2 ,
\end{equation}
since $\frac2s=2-\frac3a$; and \eqref{eq:qe-defect-scaling} is the exact
criticality relation for $\sigma$, which scales as
$\lambda^2\sigma(\lambda x,\lambda^2t)$ under the dilation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:scaling}, so that
$\int_0^{T/\lambda^2}\norm{\sigma_\lambda(t)}_a^sdt
=\lambda^{s(2-3/a)-2}\int_0^T\norm{\sigma(t)}_a^sdt$ and invariance forces
$s\bigl(2-\frac3a\bigr)=2$.  At $a=2$ one has $a'=2$, $k=6$,
$\alpha=\frac34$, $s=4$, $C_{T,2}=1$ by Plancherel (this is
$|\hat\Pi_b|\le|\hat T|_F$ in Step~1 of
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-mixed-pressure}), $B_2=B_0$ and
$C_2=\frac14\bigl(\frac34\bigr)^32^3B_0^4=\frac{27}{32}B_0^4=C_\sigma$: the
family specialises exactly to Proposition~\ref{prop:qe-defect-criterion}.

Line \eqref{eq:qe-defect-scaling} is the Ladyzhenskaya--Prodi--Serrin line
in the Beir\~ao da Veiga form, that is, the classical line for the
\emph{velocity gradient}; what is measured here is instead the single
scalar $\sigma=-\operatorname{div}w$, the divergence of the minimizing
representative.  Two things must be said about this.  To begin with, the general
family is recorded and not used: it would oblige the manuscript to carry
the two further multiplier constants $C_{T,a'}$ and $C_k$, neither of which
is among the imported facts of
\S\ref{subsec:quotient-conventions}, and nothing in the $a=2$ chain
Proposition~\ref{prop:qe-defect-criterion} --
Corollary~\ref{cor:qe-defect-producer} needs it.  Second, Theorem~\ref{de:near2} now supplies an unweighted bound of
$\norm\sigma_a$ by $\norm{\nabla u}_a$ on a fixed open interval around
$2$, under the stated additional integrability assumption on the gradient.
That extension awaits independent audit and does not settle a weighted
Calder\'on--Zygmund theorem at arbitrary exponents.  Remark~\ref{rem:qe-defect-scope}
records what this does and does not mean.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the divergence-defect criterion]
\label{rem:qe-defect-scope}
Four limitations, all of them required reading with
Corollary~\ref{cor:qe-defect-producer}.

\emph{Implication direction and the actual-branch finiteness question.}
By \eqref{eq:qdc-sigmaid},
$\norm{\sigma(t)}_2\le\frac12\norm{\nabla u(t)}_2$ at every time, hence
\[
 \int_0^\tau\norm{\sigma(t)}_2^4dt
 \le\frac1{16}\int_0^\tau Y(t)^2dt,
 \qquad Y=\norm{\nabla u}_2^2.
\]
Thus the classical gradient condition implies the defect condition, not
conversely by this estimate alone.  Earlier wording that an implied
condition is ``not weaker'' reversed that logical direction.  No strict
improvement follows merely from observing a smaller quantity.
Corollary~\ref{de:no-separation}, whose component proof awaits independent
audit, now supplies the reverse finiteness implication on actual selected
branches, with an explicit bound and without the endpoint theorem.
Consequently the earlier unresolved possibility of finite fourth-power
defect integral but infinite squared-enstrophy integral is excluded by
that proof.  The distinction is the observed scalar, not a demonstrated
strict gain in the class of trajectories certified.

\emph{The hypothesis lies in the existential-equivalence class.}  At the
quantifiers of Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain} --- for every $\nu$, every
divergence-free Schwartz datum and every horizon $H$, with the constant
depending only on those --- statement \eqref{eq:qe-defect-hypothesis} is
\emph{equivalent} to global continuation of the selected classical branch,
exactly as Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain} is by
Remark~\ref{rem:highstrain-scope} and
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highpressure} is by
Proposition~\ref{prop:existential-equivalence}.
Sufficiency is Corollary~\ref{cor:qe-defect-producer} followed by
Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-conditional} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional}.  For the converse, suppose $T_*=\infty$ for
the given datum.  Then $[0,H]$ is a compact classical interval,
$t\mapsto\norm{\sigma(t)}_2$ is Borel on it by the argument of
Corollary~\ref{cor:quotient-budgets}, and
$\norm{\sigma(t)}_2\le\frac12Y(t)^{1/2}$ with $Y$ continuous on $[0,H]$ by
(R1); hence $B_\sigma:=\int_0^H\norm{\sigma(t)}_2^4dt<\infty$ and depends
only on $\nu$, $u_0$ and $H$, so \eqref{eq:qe-defect-hypothesis} holds.
This converse assumes global continuation and supplies no method for
proving it.  Consequently the value of \eqref{eq:qe-defect-hypothesis} is
that it is a different and more concrete mechanism --- a single scalar
function of time on a known critical line --- and not that it is a weaker
assumption than the gap it produces.

\emph{What is unconditionally available falls short by a factor of two in
the time exponent.}  Corollary~\ref{cor:quotient-budgets} gives
$\int_0^\tau\norm\sigma_2^2\le\frac{E_0}{8\nu}$ for every $\tau<T_*$,
unconditionally and uniformly.  Hypothesis
\eqref{eq:qe-defect-hypothesis} asks for the \emph{fourth} power, which no
estimate in this article supplies for arbitrary data; the square bound does
not imply it even on a fixed finite interval; see the scalar example
at the end of Section~\ref{de:section}.  Naming
$\int_0^\tau\norm\sigma_2^4$ as an input function, or defining $B_\sigma$
through the unknown supremum, would assume the conclusion.

\emph{No progress toward a proof is claimed.}
Proposition~\ref{prop:qe-defect-criterion},
Corollaries~\ref{cor:qe-defect-gronwall} and
\ref{cor:qe-defect-producer} prove no unconditional regularity statement,
promote no hypothesis, and leave Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain},
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highpressure}, Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:critical} and
Theorem~\ref{def:target} exactly where they stood.  They are a conditional
producer for \eqref{eq:quotient-gap} with $\theta=0$, obtained from
Lemma~\ref{lem:qe-strain-defect} by discarding the sign of $K_u$ and by a
one-sided estimate that has no matching lower bound, and they are recorded
as such.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Low strain, the missing high-strain estimate, and its consequence}
\label{subsec:qe-highstrain}

Let $S_L$, $L\in\mathbb Z$, be the low-pass projection of
Definition~\ref{def:lp}, i.e.\ Tao's $P_{\le2^L}$ with symbol
$\varphi(\xi/2^L)$, so that $S_Lf=\kappa_L*f$ with
$\kappa_L=2^{3L}\kappa(2^L\,\cdot)$ and $\hat\kappa=\varphi$; the symbol
$\psi=\varphi-\varphi(2\,\cdot)$ and the homogeneous blocks $\Delta_j$ of
Definition~\ref{def:lp} are not used here, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:lp-coincide} reconciles $S_L$ with $\sum_{j\le L}\Delta_j$
on $L^2$.  Since $\kappa_L\in\mathcal S(\R^3)$, $S_L$ acts componentwise
on vector fields and $\partial_kS_Lf=(\partial_k\kappa_L)*f$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:lowpass-kernel}).  We use the Bernstein estimate of
Lemma~\ref{lem:bernstein}: for $f\in L^2(\R^3;\R^3)$, $S_Lf$ has a
$C^\infty$ representative and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-bernstein}
 \sup_{x\in\R^3}\bigl|\nabla S_Lf(x)\bigr|_F\le C_B\,2^{5L/2}\norm f_2 ,
 \qquad C_B=2\pi\norm{\,|\xi|\varphi\,}_2\le16\pi\sqrt{2\pi/5},
\end{equation}
and $t\mapsto\nabla S_Lu(t)\in C([0,T];L^\infty)$ whenever
$u\in C([0,T];L^2)$.

\begin{remark}[How the two balances differ]\label{rem:distance-balance}
Subtracting \eqref{eq:pressure-balance} from the integrated
\eqref{eq:quotient-evolution}, both of which hold on every compact
subinterval of $[0,T_*)$ for the branch of
Proposition~\ref{prop:localtheory}, shows that the nonnegative difference
$\frac13\norm{u}_3^3-\mathcal Q(u)$ is absolutely continuous with
\[
 \frac d{dt}\Bigl(\tfrac13\norm{u(t)}_3^3-\mathcal Q(u(t))\Bigr)
 =P_3(t)-K(t)+\nu\bigl(D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))-D_3(u(t))\bigr)
\]
for almost every $t$.  The two dissipations are different objects:
$D_3$ of Definition~\ref{def:D3P3} is built from $u$, while
$D_{\mathcal Q}$ of Definition~\ref{def:qe-dissipation} is built from the
minimizing representative $w$, and the two coincide only where $q=0$.  The
identity records exactly how the pressure work and the strain work differ,
and supplies no bound for either.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Low-strain Gronwall coefficient]\label{lem:quotient-lowstrain}
Let $L\in\mathbb Z$ and $[0,T]\subset[0,T_*)$.  For $t\in[0,T]$ put
$v(t)=S_Lu(t)$ and
\[
 K_{\rm low}(t)=-\int_{\R^3}q(t)\cdot\bigl((A(t)\cdot\nabla)v(t)\bigr)dx,
 \qquad
 K_L(t)=-\int_{\R^3}q(t)\cdot\bigl((A(t)\cdot\nabla)(u(t)-v(t))\bigr)dx .
\]
Then $K_{\rm low}$ and $K_L$ are continuous on $[0,T]$,
\eqref{eq:quotient-evolution} reads
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-split}
 \frac d{dt}\mathcal Q(u(t))+\nu D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))=K_L(t)+K_{\rm low}(t),
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-lowstrain}
 |K_{\rm low}(t)|\le M_L\,\mathcal Q(u(t)),
 \qquad
 M_L:=3\,(1+C_{\mathbb P})\,C_B\,2^{5L/2}\norm{u_0}_2 .
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix $t$ and write $u=u(t)$, $v=S_Lu$, $q=q(t)$, $A=A(t)$, $w=w(t)$.  By
(R1), $u\in L^2$, so by \eqref{eq:qe-bernstein} $Dv$ is continuous and
bounded, $D(u-v)$ is bounded as well, and both integrals converge
absolutely; the splitting $Du=Dv+D(u-v)$ is exact, which gives
\eqref{eq:qe-split} from \eqref{eq:quotient-evolution}.  Pointwise, by
\eqref{eq:qe-jacobian} and \eqref{eq:qe-frobenius},
$|q\cdot((A\cdot\nabla)v)|=|q\cdot(Dv\,A)|\le|q|\,|Dv|_F\,|A|$, and by
\eqref{eq:qe-bernstein} and Proposition~\ref{prop:energy},
\[
 \sup_x|Dv(x)|_F\le C_B2^{5L/2}\norm{u(t)}_2\le C_B2^{5L/2}\norm{u_0}_2 .
\]
H\"older's inequality with exponents $\infty$, $3$, $3/2$, then
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-coercive} ($\norm q_3\le(1+C_{\mathbb P})\norm w_3$,
applicable because $u(t)\in L^3$ is solenoidal, as shown after
Lemma~\ref{lem:qe-embedding}) and
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-minimizer}(b) ($\norm A_{3/2}=\norm w_3^2$,
$\norm w_3^3=3\mathcal Q(u)$), give
\[
 |K_{\rm low}(t)|
 \le\sup_x|Dv|_F\;\norm q_3\norm A_{3/2}
 \le C_B2^{5L/2}\norm{u_0}_2\,(1+C_{\mathbb P})\norm w_3\cdot\norm w_3^2
 =M_L\,\mathcal Q(u(t)) .
\]
Continuity of $K_{\rm low}$ follows from the three-term estimate in the
proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-evolution}, with $Dv$ in place of
$Du$, since $t\mapsto Dv(t)=\nabla S_Lu(t)$ belongs to $C([0,T];L^\infty)$
by Lemma~\ref{lem:bernstein} and $u\in C([0,T];L^2)$ (R1).  Then $K_L$ is
continuous as the difference of the continuous right side of
\eqref{eq:quotient-evolution} and $K_{\rm low}$.
\end{proof}

The remaining term $K_L$ is the only part of the transport work not
controlled by an input-dependent coefficient.  A sufficient
\emph{unproved} producer is the following.

\begin{hypothesis}[Signed high-strain absorption]\label{hyp:highstrain}
For an integer $L$ and $t\in[0,T_*)$ let, as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-lowstrain},
\[
 K_L(t)=-\int_{\R^3}q(t)\cdot\bigl((A(t)\cdot\nabla)(u(t)-S_Lu(t))\bigr)dx ,
\]
where $q(t)$, $A(t)$ are the minimizer data of
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-minimizer}(b) at $u(t)$ and $S_L$ is the low-pass
projection of Definition~\ref{def:lp}; let $D_{\mathcal Q}$ be as in
Definition~\ref{def:qe-dissipation}.  There exists a fixed
$\theta\in[0,1]$ such that, for every $\nu>0$, divergence-free Schwartz
datum $u_0$ (with $(u,p)$ and $T_*$ the branch of that datum), and
$0<H<\infty$, there exist an integer $L=L(\nu,u_0,H)$ and
a finite nonnegative $A_{\rm input}=A_{\rm input}(\nu,u_0,H,L)$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:quotient-gap}
 \int_0^\tau K_L(t)\,dt
 \le\theta\nu\int_0^\tau D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))\,dt+A_{\rm input}
\end{equation}
for every $0<\tau<\min\{H,T_*\}$.  The same $L$ and $A_{\rm input}$ must
work for the entire interval, and $A_{\rm input}$ depends only on the
initial datum, viscosity, horizon, and input-selected cutoff.
\end{hypothesis}

As with Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highpressure}, the existential assertion is
nontrivial: the left side could be unbounded as $\tau\uparrow T_*$.  A proof
must produce $A_{\rm input}$ without assuming the continuation bound it is
meant to imply; a remainder defined through the unknown supremum is
circular.  Because $\theta=1$ is permitted, the value of $\theta$ plays no
role below; $\theta<1$ would additionally retain
$(1-\theta)\nu\int_0^\tau D_{\mathcal Q}$.

\begin{remark}[The Gronwall term and the frequency split are cosmetic]
\label{rem:highstrain-normalisation}
Two features of Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain} carry no content.  Since
$\mathcal Q\leq\frac13\norm u_3^3$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-coercive},
H\"older in time with exponents $\frac43$ and $4$ and \eqref{eq:L4L3} give,
for every $\tau<\min\{H,T_*\}$,
\[
 \int_0^\tau\mathcal Q(u(t))\,dt
 \leq\tfrac13\Bigl(\int_0^\tau\norm{u(t)}_3^4dt\Bigr)^{3/4}\tau^{1/4}
 \leq\tfrac13H^{1/4}\Bigl(\frac{3C_S^2\norm{u_0}_2^4}{2\nu}\Bigr)^{3/4},
\]
which depends only on the input.  Consequently any additional term
$M\int_0^\tau\mathcal Q\,dt$ on the right of \eqref{eq:quotient-gap}, with $M$
input-determined, is absorbable into $A_{\rm input}$; and by
\eqref{eq:qe-lowstrain} the same bound applies to
$\bigl|\int_0^\tau K_{\rm low}\bigr|\leq M_L\int_0^\tau\mathcal Q$, so
\eqref{eq:quotient-gap} for $K_L$ with a datum-selected $L$ is equivalent, up
to an input-only change of $A_{\rm input}$, to the same inequality for the
full $K=K_L+K_{\rm low}$ with no frequency split at all.  Unlike the pressure
route, where Proposition~\ref{prop:lowpressure} is a genuine lemma, the
low-frequency work of the quotient route is free.  This normalises the
statement and leaves its content untouched; Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain}
is kept in the $L$-form because that is what
Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-conditional} consumes.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional critical bound from high-strain absorption]
\label{prop:quotient-conditional}
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain} implies Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:critical}, with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qe-M}
 M(\nu,u_0,H)
 =C_{\mathbb P}\bigl(\norm{u_0}_3^3+3A_{\rm input}\bigr)^{1/3}
 \exp\Bigl(\tfrac13M_LH\Bigr),
\end{equation}
where $L$ and $A_{\rm input}$ are those provided by
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain} and $M_L$ is the constant of
\eqref{eq:qe-lowstrain}.  Consequently, by Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional},
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain} implies Theorem~\ref{def:target}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Fix $\nu$, $u_0$, $H$, and let $\theta$, $L$, $A_{\rm input}$ be given by
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain}.  Let $0<\tau_1<\min\{H,T_*\}$; then
$[0,\tau_1]$ is a compact subinterval of $[0,T_*)$, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-evolution} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-lowstrain} apply on it.  Put $y(t)=\mathcal Q(u(t))$,
which is nonnegative and belongs to $C^1([0,\tau_1])$; in particular $y$ is
absolutely continuous and the fundamental theorem of calculus applies.
Integrating \eqref{eq:qe-split} over $[0,\tau]$ for $0<\tau\le\tau_1$, all
integrands being continuous,
\[
 y(\tau)+\nu\int_0^\tau D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))\,dt
 =y(0)+\int_0^\tau K_L(t)\,dt+\int_0^\tau K_{\rm low}(t)\,dt .
\]
By \eqref{eq:quotient-gap} and \eqref{eq:qe-lowstrain},
\[
 y(\tau)+(1-\theta)\nu\int_0^\tau D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))\,dt
 \le y(0)+A_{\rm input}+M_L\int_0^\tau y(t)\,dt .
\]
Since $\theta\le1$ and $D_{\mathcal Q}\ge0$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-heatsign}), the second term on the left is
nonnegative and may be dropped; the resulting inequality also holds at
$\tau=0$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:qe-gronwall} with $a=y(0)+A_{\rm input}$ and
$M=M_L$ gives
$y(\tau)\le\bigl(y(0)+A_{\rm input}\bigr)e^{M_L\tau}\le\bigl(y(0)+A_{\rm input}\bigr)e^{M_LH}$
for all $\tau\in[0,\tau_1]$.  Since $\tau_1<\min\{H,T_*\}$ was arbitrary,
the bound holds on $[0,\min\{H,T_*\})$.  By
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-coercive}, estimate \eqref{eq:cp-coercive},
$y(0)\le\frac13\norm{u_0}_3^3$ and
$\norm{u(\tau)}_3^3\le3C_{\mathbb P}^3y(\tau)$ ($u(\tau)\in L^3$ is
solenoidal), so
\[
 \sup_{0\le\tau<\min\{H,T_*\}}\norm{u(\tau)}_3^3
 \le C_{\mathbb P}^3\bigl(\norm{u_0}_3^3+3A_{\rm input}\bigr)e^{M_LH},
\]
which is \eqref{eq:missing} with $M$ as in \eqref{eq:qe-M}.  The last
sentence is Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the high-strain hypothesis]\label{rem:highstrain-scope}
Integration of \eqref{eq:quotient-evolution} and Gronwall would then bound
$\mathcal Q$, hence $L^3$, through the putative endpoint; this is exactly
Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-conditional}.  At the quantifiers of
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain}, the hypothesis is equivalent to global
continuation of the selected classical branch, as for
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highpressure}.  The forward direction is
Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-conditional} together with
Theorem~\ref{thm:continuation}.  Conversely, if $T_*=\infty$ for the given
datum, take $L=0$ and $\theta=0$: $K_0$ is continuous on the compact
classical interval $[0,H]$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-lowstrain}, so
$A_{\rm input}=\int_0^H|K_0(t)|dt$ is finite and \eqref{eq:quotient-gap}
holds.  This converse assumes global continuation and supplies no method
for proving it.  Neither \eqref{eq:quotient-gap} nor a quantitative
dissipation mechanism for it has been proved.  Bounding $K_L$ by
$\norm{\nabla(u-S_Lu)}_\infty\mathcal Q(u)$ merely renames the missing
high-frequency control.  The two routes are moreover ordered: subtracting
\eqref{eq:pressure-balance} from the integrated \eqref{eq:quotient-evolution}
shows that Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:absorption} implies \eqref{eq:quotient-gap}
directly, with $\theta=1$ and
$A_{\rm input}=A(\nu,u_0,H)+\frac13\norm{u_0}_3^3$ plus the input quantity of
Remark~\ref{rem:highstrain-normalisation}, so the high-strain hypothesis is
not harder than the high-pressure one.  This is a quantitative form of the
composition of Corollary~\ref{cor:absorption-consequence},
Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional} and the converse above, not a new implication.
  This is an alternative to the pressure route; no
novelty or Millennium solution is asserted.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[No monotone mechanism and no instantaneous energy-only
absorption]\label{rem:no-monotone}
Two mechanisms that Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain} might have been thought to
follow from are excluded outright.  There exist $U,h\in C_c^\infty(\R^3)^3$ with
$\nabla\cdot U=\nabla\cdot h=0$, $U\neq0$, and a finite constant $C_*$ with
\[
  \bigl|K(U+\varepsilon h)+\varepsilon\norm U_3^3\bigr|\leq C_*|\varepsilon|^{3/2}
  \qquad(|\varepsilon|\leq1),
\]
so that $K$ takes both signs on smooth compactly supported solenoidal fields;
$U$ is an azimuthal swirl, for which $\nabla\cdot(|U|U)=0$ and hence $w(U)=U$,
and $h$ is a compactly supported solenoidal field agreeing with the harmonic
gradient $(x,y,-2z)$ near $\operatorname{supp}U$, the estimate coming from a
competing gradient supported away from $U$ rather than from any derivative of
the minimiser.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-scaling} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-evolution} it follows that for every $\nu>0$ and
every $E>0$ there is a divergence-free $v_0\in C_c^\infty(\R^3)^3$ with
$\norm{v_0}_2^2=E$ whose classical branch satisfies
$\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal Q(v(t))|_{0^+}>0$, and that
\[
  \sup\bigl\{K(v)-\beta\nu D_{\mathcal Q}(v):v\in C_c^\infty(\R^3)^3,\
  \nabla\cdot v=0,\ \norm v_2^2=E\bigr\}=+\infty
\]
for every $\beta\geq0$.  Consequently no strictly increasing function of
$\mathcal Q$ decreases along every classical trajectory, and no finite
$B(E,\nu,\beta)$ makes $K(v)\leq\beta\nu D_{\mathcal Q}(v)+B(E,\nu,\beta)$ hold
pointwise at fixed energy.

This bears on neither Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain} nor
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highpressure}: those are spacetime statements with
remainders depending on the entire datum, and the first concerns $K_L$ rather
than $K$.  Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-conditional} is untouched, since it
consumes the spacetime hypothesis and not a monotonicity statement, and
Remark~\ref{rem:qe-evolution-scope} already declined to assert a sign.  The
certificate is qualitative: for the fields as written the admissible
$\varepsilon$ is far below any scale of numerical use.

The exclusion survives time integration, and it reaches the high-frequency
work $K_L$ for every fixed cutoff.  For every $\nu>0$, every $E>0$, every
$H>0$, every $\beta\geq0$ and every fixed integer $L$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:no-monotone-spacetime}
\begin{split}
 \sup\Bigl\{\int_0^\tau&\bigl(K_L(t)-\beta\nu D_{\mathcal Q}(u(t))\bigr)dt:
 u_0\in C_c^\infty(\R^3)^3,\ \nabla\cdot u_0=0,\\
 &\norm{u_0}_2^2=E,\ 0<\tau<\min\{H,T_*\}\Bigr\}=+\infty ,
\end{split}
\end{equation}
where each such $u_0$ is Schwartz, $(u,p)$ and $T_*=T_*(\nu,u_0)$ are its
branch at viscosity $\nu$, and $K_L$, $D_{\mathcal Q}$ are as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-lowstrain} and Definition~\ref{def:qe-dissipation};
that these are defined along the branch is the regularity package
Proposition~\ref{prop:localtheory}(iii),(iv), obtained there through
Lemma~\ref{lem:upgrade}, which is imported and not re-derived here, and that
the strain work is the transport work is
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-transport}, used as it stands.  Relative to the
instantaneous statement above these are exactly two strengthenings: from a
single time to a time integral, and from $K$ to $K_L$ for every fixed $L$.
The witnesses are built from the same two fields.  Fix $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$
so small that the display above, with $\varepsilon$ replaced by
$-\varepsilon$, gives
$k:=K(V)\geq\varepsilon\norm U_3^3-C_*\varepsilon^{3/2}>0$ for
$V=U-\varepsilon h$; for $0<\mu\leq1$ let $v^\mu$ be the branch of the fixed
datum $V$ at viscosity $\mu$, and for $b\geq\nu$ put
\[
 u_b(t,x)=b\lambda\,v^{\nu/b}(b\lambda^2t,\lambda x),
 \qquad\lambda=b^2\norm V_2^2/E ,
\]
so that $u_b$ solves \eqref{eq:NS} at viscosity exactly $\nu$, with datum
$b\mathcal D_\lambda V\in C_c^\infty(\R^3)^3$ and
$\norm{b\mathcal D_\lambda V}_2^2=E$.  An $H^6$ energy estimate that discards
the viscous term rather than dividing by it, together with
Proposition~\ref{prop:localtheory}(v), gives one $T_0>0$ and one $B<\infty$,
both depending only on $V$, such that every $v^\mu$ exists past $T_0$ with
$\sup_{0<\mu\leq1}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T_0}\norm{v^\mu(s)}_{H^6}\leq B$; since
$D_{\mathcal Q}(v)\leq\norm{w(v)}_3^2\norm{\Delta v}_3\leq\norm v_3^2\norm{\Delta v}_3$
by Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-minimizer}(b), and since the three-term H\"older
estimate in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-evolution}, with
\eqref{eq:cp-strong} and \eqref{eq:cp-continuity} and the embedding of $H^4$
in $L^3$ and in $C^1$ with bounded derivatives, bounds $|K(v)-K(V)|$ by a
constant multiple of $\norm{v-V}_{H^4}^{1/2}+\norm{v-V}_{H^4}$ on bounded
subsets of $H^4$,
there are one $s_0\in(0,T_0)$ and one $D_{\max}<\infty$, both independent of
$\mu$, with $K(v^\mu(s))\geq k/2$ and $D_{\mathcal Q}(v^\mu(s))\leq D_{\max}$
for $0\leq s\leq s_0$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-scaling} and the
homogeneities $K(b\mathcal D_\lambda v)=b^4\lambda^2K(v)$,
$D_{\mathcal Q}(b\mathcal D_\lambda v)=b^3\lambda^2D_{\mathcal Q}(v)$, the
change of variables is an identity rather than an estimate:
\[
 \int_0^{\tau_b}\bigl(K(u_b(t))-\beta\nu D_{\mathcal Q}(u_b(t))\bigr)dt
 =b^3\int_0^{s_0}\bigl(K(v^{\nu/b}(s))-\beta(\nu/b)D_{\mathcal Q}(v^{\nu/b}(s))\bigr)ds
 \geq\tfrac14ks_0b^3
\]
once $b$ is large enough that $\beta(\nu/b)D_{\max}\leq k/4$, where
$\tau_b=s_0\bigl(E/\norm V_2^2\bigr)^2b^{-5}$ lies in
$(0,\min\{H,T_*\})$ for all large $b$ because $s_0<T_0$.  The interval of
positivity shrinks like $b^{-5}$ while the integrand is of size $b^8$, and
their product diverges; this is the whole of the passage from the
instantaneous statement to the integrated one.  The cutoff is then applied at
the original scale and never transported: $L$ being fixed before $b$,
\eqref{eq:qe-lowstrain} gives
$\bigl|\int_0^{\tau_b}K_{\rm low}\bigr|\leq M_L\int_0^{\tau_b}\mathcal Q(u_b(t))dt$
with $M_L=3(1+C_{\mathbb P})C_B2^{5L/2}\sqrt E$, and
Remark~\ref{rem:highstrain-normalisation} bounds the last integral by a
quantity depending only on $E$, $\nu$ and $H$, so subtracting it leaves
\eqref{eq:no-monotone-spacetime}.  Consequently, taking $\beta=\theta$, no
finite remainder depending only on $(E,\nu,H,L,\theta)$ can serve in
\eqref{eq:quotient-gap},
and neither can a rule selecting the cutoff from those scalars alone.  Of
the two reasons given above for why the instantaneous certificate does not
bear on Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain}, only one survives here: this is a
spacetime statement, and it does reach $K_L$, the family never being
transferred to the cutoff, which would fail because $S_L$ is not equivariant
under the dilations $\mathcal D_\lambda$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-scaling}.  What remains is the whole-datum
dependence, and it is decisive: Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain} selects both
$L$ and $A_{\rm input}$ from the entire datum, whereas the family above uses
a different datum for each $b$, with $\norm{u_b(0)}_3=b\norm V_3\to\infty$
and $\tau_b\to0$.  So neither Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain} nor
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highpressure} is touched, no critical norm is unbounded
along any one trajectory, and no singular solution is produced; the family
is built from the fields of the certificate above, so the qualitative caveat
recorded there applies to it as well, and no novelty is claimed for the
construction or for the scaling mechanism behind it.  One further
consequence should be recorded, independent of the construction: the
absolute route left open by \eqref{eq:qdc-KY} requires an unproved critical
producer, not a consequence of the energy estimate alone. A finite input-only
bound on $\int_0^\tau Y(t)^2dt$, uniform in $\tau<\min\{H,T_*\}$,
implies the Ladyzhenskaya--Prodi--Serrin condition $u\in L^4((0,\tau);L^6)$ ($\norm u_6\leq C_SY^{1/2}$ and
$\frac36+\frac24=1$), and inserting
$|\langle(u\cdot\nabla)u,\Delta u\rangle|\leq\norm u_6\norm{\nabla u}_3\norm{\Delta u}_2$
with
$\norm{\nabla u}_3\leq\norm{\nabla u}_2^{1/2}\norm{\nabla u}_6^{1/2}
\leq C_S^{1/2}Y^{1/4}\norm{\Delta u}_2^{1/2}$ (the Sobolev and Plancherel
chain of Step~2 of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:serrin-enstrophy}) into
\eqref{eq:serrin-enstrophy-identity}, absorbing by Young's inequality with
exponents $\frac43$ and $4$, and applying Gronwall gives
$Y(t)\leq Y(0)\exp\bigl(C\nu^{-3}C_S^4\int_0^\tau Y^2\bigr)$ with an
absolute constant $C$, hence
$\sup_{t<\min\{H,T_*\}}\norm{u(t)}_{H^1}<\infty$, which already yields
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:critical}, by interpolating $L^3$ between $L^2$ and
$L^6$, and, by
Proposition~\ref{prop:localtheory}(v), $T_*=\infty$ when this bound is
available for every finite horizon. Deriving that bound would establish
the targeted continuation, so it remains a legitimate producer to pursue;
it cannot be inserted as an already available estimate.
\end{remark}

\section{Proof boundary}

\emph{Proved here, in full.}  The local theory package
(Proposition~\ref{prop:localtheory} with Corollary~\ref{cor:Lq},
Lemma~\ref{lem:global-smooth} and the auxiliary lemmas of
\S\ref{sec:localtheory}) is derived from the imported statements listed
below; every deduction from them, including the restriction and
regularity-upgrade lemmas, the identification of mild with classical
solutions, the pressure convention (Lemma~\ref{lem:pressure-convention}),
and the viscosity normalisation (Lemma~\ref{lem:nu-scaling}), is proved.
On that branch the manuscript-owned results are the energy identity
(Proposition~\ref{prop:energy}), the scaling law and the interpolation
mismatch (Proposition~\ref{prop:scaling}, Remark~\ref{rem:mismatch}), the
cubic enstrophy inequality (Proposition~\ref{prop:enstrophy}), the scalar
obstruction (Proposition~\ref{prop:ode}), the integrated cubic pressure
balance (Proposition~\ref{prop:pressure}) with the calculus lemmas behind
it, the low-frequency pressure-work bound
(Proposition~\ref{prop:lowpressure}) with its Littlewood--Paley kernel
estimates and the Bernstein instance (Lemma~\ref{lem:bernstein}), the
absorption split (Lemma~\ref{lem:absorption-split}) and its consequence
(Corollary~\ref{cor:absorption-consequence}), the existential equivalence
of the signed tail hypothesis with global continuation
(Proposition~\ref{prop:existential-equivalence}), the Leray--Hopf
membership of the classical branch (Lemma~\ref{lem:leray-hopf}) with the
density and Sobolev lemmas it needs, the Serrin-type enstrophy bound
(Lemma~\ref{lem:serrin-enstrophy}), the endpoint continuation theorem
(Theorem~\ref{thm:continuation}), the conditional Clay alternative~A
(Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional}), and the whole of the quotient section:
the functional $\mathcal Q$ of Definition~\ref{def:quotient}, its
coercivity (Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-coercive}), scaling and heat
monotonicity (Lemmas~\ref{lem:quotient-scaling} and
\ref{lem:quotient-heat}), its derivative
(Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-derivative}), the pressure-free evolution
identity (Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-evolution}), the low-strain bound
(Lemma~\ref{lem:quotient-lowstrain}), and the conditional critical bound
that follows from the high-strain hypothesis
(Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-conditional}).

\emph{Imported, not reproved.}  Four literature statements are used, each
transcribed where it is invoked: Theorem~5.4 of Tao
\cite[pp.~52--53]{Tao2013} on local existence for $H^1$ data
(Theorem~\ref{thm:tao54}), Corollary~5.8 of Tao \cite[pp.~56--57]{Tao2013}
on the per-horizon blow-up dichotomy, from which the maximal time is
constructed in Proposition~\ref{prop:localtheory}
(Theorem~\ref{thm:tao58}), together with
its companion Corollary~4.3 \cite[p.~47]{Tao2013}
identifying almost smooth $H^1$ solutions as mild solutions with the
normalised pressure (Theorem~\ref{thm:tao43}), and Theorem~1.3 of Escauriaza, Seregin, and
\v Sver\'ak \cite[p.~214]{ESS2003} on $L^{3}$-bounded Leray--Hopf solutions
(Theorem~\ref{thm:ess}).  The backward-uniqueness proof behind the last is
not reproduced.  Theorem~4 of Gallagher, Koch, and Planchon
\cite{GKP2013} is corroboration only and is not used
(Remark~\ref{rem:gkp}); the standard textbook facts collected in the
conventions are cited at chapter or theorem level and not reproved.
The quotient section also imports the $L^p$ boundedness of the Leray
projection for $p\in\{3,3/2\}$ ((F5) of
\S\ref{subsec:quotient-conventions}, Calder\'on--Zygmund theory
\cite{Stein1970}), used in Lemma~\ref{lem:leray}(b).

\emph{Open.}  Two hypotheses are stated and neither is proved:
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highpressure}, the signed high-frequency pressure
tail, which with the proved low-frequency bound is what remains of
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:absorption}; and Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:highstrain}, its
strain-side alternative, whose inequality is \eqref{eq:quotient-gap}.
Either one implies Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:critical} and hence
Theorem~\ref{def:target}, by
Corollary~\ref{cor:absorption-consequence}(ii) and
Proposition~\ref{prop:quotient-conditional} respectively.  Both are also
equivalent, at their own existential quantifiers, to the global
continuation they are meant to produce
(Proposition~\ref{prop:existential-equivalence},
Remark~\ref{rem:highstrain-scope}), so neither may be treated as a
technical lemma.  No high-pressure, high-strain, critical, or absorption
estimate is asserted anywhere in this paper, and the conditional theorems
are not a solution of the Navier--Stokes Millennium problem.

\emph{Additional missing-bound reductions, review pending.}
The fourth-power defect condition \eqref{eq:qe-defect-hypothesis} is also
unproved. Section~\ref{sd:section} sharpens its coefficient by an exact
signed cancellation and replaces it, as a sufficient input, by an
endpoint-uniform integral bound for a one-sided quadratic-form rate.
Corollary~\ref{sd:missing-bound-consumer} gives the original defect bound
explicitly from that new input; it does not establish the input itself.
The new component and its nonendpoint quotient-clock suffix await
independent mathematical audit. Section~\ref{ss:section} additionally
proves that the defect has zero average on every speed shell, derives
a residual after removal of speed-only functions, and exploits scalar
corrections that leave the signed work unchanged. The endpoint-uniform
bound on the resulting combined rate remains unproved.
No terminal or open graph node is promoted.
Section~\ref{st:section} now justifies differentiation of a finite,
regularized shell residual through the natural variable. This does not
justify differentiation of the full moving projection or bound the
new evolution drivers uniformly at the endpoint.
Section~\ref{mr:section} differentiates the cubic representative in its
fixed weighted space and the natural variable strongly in $L^2$, even at
zeros. Pullback by the actual flow isolates an exact diffusion--strain
action after removal of rigid rotation. This is not differentiation of
the full sharp speed projection, and it does not bound the action or
nonlocal commutator at the endpoint. Independent audit remains pending.
